I rechecked it top to bottom. For the LTX travel plan itself, the implementation is complete.

The key pieces are in place in [settings.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/settings.ts), [VideoTravelSettingsProvider.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/providers/VideoTravelSettingsProvider.tsx), and [requestBody.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/ShotEditor/services/generateVideo/requestBody.ts). I also added explicit coverage for the last two seams that were only implied before: model-switch persistence in [VideoTravelSettingsProvider.test.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/providers/VideoTravelSettingsProvider.test.tsx) and LTX UI gating in [MotionControl.test.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/MotionControl.test.tsx).

Verification is solid:
- `npx tsc --noEmit` passed.
- `npm run build -- --mode development` passed.
- The focused LTX/travel regression slice passed: `10` files, `224` tests.
- I checked the referenced worker branch directly. The worker accepts `ltx2_22B` and `ltx2_22B_distilled`, reads `num_inference_steps` and `guidance_scale` from orchestrator details, uses 8N+1 quantization for LTX, resolves `model_type='i2v'` safely, and still anchors `image_end` for LTX in [task_registry.py](/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py), [orchestrator.py](/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/source/task_handlers/travel/orchestrator.py), [segment_processor.py](/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/source/task_handlers/travel/segment_processor.py), and [ltx2.py](/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx2.py).

The only thing that is not globally green is the repo’s pre-existing full-suite state. `npm test` still ends with `13 failed | 813 passed`, with failures unrelated to this plan, for example [usePersistentToolState.test.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/hooks/__tests__/usePersistentToolState.test.ts), [segmentOutputsQueries.test.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/hooks/segments/__tests__/segmentOutputsQueries.test.ts), and an existing expectation mismatch in [pairPayload.test.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/ShotEditor/services/generateVideo/pairPayload.test.ts). That does not indicate an incomplete LTX implementation, but the branch is not fully clean outside this task.

So the answer is: for the plan scope, yes, it is complete. The remaining risk is not missing client work; it is the worker repo still lacking an LTX travel-segment matrix case, even though the contract itself now checks out.

> AGENT

I was not 100% confident before this pass. I found one real issue and fixed it.

The issue was in the shared request builder: WAN requests could still carry `num_inference_steps` and `guidance_scale`, which violated the intended WAN payload contract. I fixed that in [requestBody.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/ShotEditor/services/generateVideo/requestBody.ts) so those fields are now LTX-only, and added a regression assertion in [requestBody.test.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/ShotEditor/services/generateVideo/requestBody.test.ts). I also cleaned up a couple of lingering raw model unions to use `SelectedModel` in [useGenerationController.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/ShotEditor/controllers/useGenerationController.ts) and [useShotEditorLayoutModel.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/ShotEditor/controllers/useShotEditorLayoutModel.ts), and normalized one `startsWith('ltx')` call to `isLtxModel()` in [generateVideoService.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/ShotEditor/services/generateVideoService.ts).

After that, I re-ran the focused verification:
- `npx tsc --noEmit` passed
- the focused travel/LTX suite passed: `11` files, `227` tests
- the added persistence/UI-gating tests passed
- the worker branch contract still checks out for `ltx2_22B` and `ltx2_22B_distilled`

Sense-check result:
- Missed: yes, one WAN payload regression; now fixed
- Over-engineered: nothing substantial; the per-model substate in the provider is justified and now covered by test
- Broke anything: not in the touched LTX/travel surface based on the focused suite
- Remaining repo state: full `npm test` still has unrelated pre-existing failures outside this task

So my answer now is: the plan implementation is complete, and the second-pass review found and closed the one meaningful gap I was able to detect. The only remaining caveat is the same external one: the worker repo still does not appear to have a dedicated `travel_orchestrator -> LTX` matrix case, even though the runtime contract matches.

> AGENT

The crash is fixed in [BatchSettingsForm.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/BatchSettingsForm.tsx).

Cause: `selectedModel` was being used as a direct key into `MODEL_DEFAULTS`, and at runtime an undefined or stale value could still reach that component. I added a defensive fallback so it now coerces invalid values to `'wan-2.2'` before reading `frameStep`, `fps`, or LTX defaults.

Verification:
- `npx tsc --noEmit` passed
- focused UI tests passed:

> AGENT

**Findings**
- The split was not fully elegant. A stale or invalid `selectedModel` could still leak through runtime state and crash model switching. I fixed that by adding a single coercion path in [settings.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/settings.ts#L96) and using it across the travel UI and request builder, including [VideoTravelSettingsProvider.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/providers/VideoTravelSettingsProvider.tsx#L115) and [requestBody.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/ShotEditor/services/generateVideo/requestBody.ts#L75).
- The LoRA modal had a real architectural break: [CommunityLorasTab.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/domains/lora/components/LoraSelectorModal/components/CommunityLorasTab.tsx#L35) was treating a modal-state hook like a full filtering engine, which is what led to the `loraType.toLowerCase` crash. I moved the actual search/filter/sort/pagination logic into the tab and hardened [filter-utils.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/domains/lora/components/LoraSelectorModal/utils/filter-utils.ts#L3) so non-string values are harmless. I also added the missing `LTX` filter option.
- The current model split is now acceptable, but still not the cleanest possible abstraction. It is a flat string union with family behavior inferred by naming (`isLtxModel`) rather than a richer capability registry. That is fine for `WAN 2.2`, `LTX 2.3`, and `LTX 2.3 Fast`, but if you add more families it will start to creak.

**Architecture Verdict**
- After the fixes, the LTX/WAN split is coherent enough for current scope. The defaults and per-model persisted substate now live in one place in [settings.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/settings.ts#L96), and switching variants preserves the right fields in [VideoTravelSettingsProvider.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/providers/VideoTravelSettingsProvider.tsx#L115).
- I do not think you are missing any mandatory LTX settings right now. The worker’s structure-guidance contract is already canonical and model-agnostic: `target`, `preprocessing`, `strength`, plus optional Uni3C fields like `step_window` and `frame_policy` in [/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/source/core/params/structure_guidance.py](/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/source/core/params/structure_guidance.py#L92). LTX prompt-type resolution also happens server-side in [/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/source/task_handlers/travel/segment_processor.py](/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/source/task_handlers/travel/segment_processor.py#L138). So the frontend is not missing required LTX-only controls; it is only omitting optional advanced structure-guidance knobs.
- `97` frames does make sense for LTX. Your defaults are explicitly `frameStep: 8`, `fps: 24`, `frames: 97` in [settings.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/settings.ts#L104), and the worker UI also defaults non-WAN video length to `97` in [/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/Wan2GP/wgp.py](/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/Wan2GP/wgp.py#L10337). `97 = 8*12 + 1`, so it matches the LTX frame quantization rule and gives about `4.04s`. It is a sensible default, not a magic fixed requirement.

**Verification**
- `npx tsc --noEmit` passed.
- `npx vitest run --config config/testing/vitest.config.ts ...` passed for the touched slice: `5` files, `36` tests.
- `npm run build:dev` passed. The remaining warnings were pre-existing bundle/CSS warnings, not from these changes.
- I also ran a targeted `desloppify scan --path src/tools/travel-between-images`; it completed analysis and reported substantial existing coupling/structural debt before crashing while serializing its own state. That lines up with the conclusion above: functional now, but not yet “beautiful” if you keep adding model families.

So the direct answer is: no, it was not elegantly engineered enough before this pass. It is materially better now, the concrete bugs are fixed, and I do not see a missing required LTX setting. The main remaining design debt is that model family/variant capability is still encoded as string conventions instead of an explicit model registry.

I found the main architectural wrinkle. The worker has a clean internal abstraction for structure guidance, but LTX control-mode selection is still a separate concern encoded as `video_prompt_type` letters like `PVG`, `DVG`, `EVG`, or `VG`. > AGENT

I checked the worker branch directly at [banodoco/Reigh-Worker `feat/worker-matrix-hardening`](https://github.com/banodoco/Reigh-Worker/tree/feat/worker-matrix-hardening).

**Duration**
- No, pair duration is not definitely fixed at `97` frames.
- In travel orchestration, each requested segment length is quantized to the model’s valid `step*N+1` size, then passed through as that segment’s `video_length` in [orchestrator.py](/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/source/task_handlers/travel/orchestrator.py#L705), [orchestrator.py](/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/source/task_handlers/travel/orchestrator.py#L764), and [task_registry.py](/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py#L601).
- For LTX-2, the valid rule is `8N+1`, with minimum `17`, in [ltx2_handler.py](/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx2_handler.py#L152).
- `97` is just a common fallback/default in the generic WGP UI for non-WAN models in [wgp.py](/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/Wan2GP/wgp.py#L10337), and both LTX defaults JSONs on this branch actually use `241` in [ltx2_22B.json](/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/Wan2GP/defaults/ltx2_22B.json#L21) and [ltx2_22B_distilled.json](/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/Wan2GP/defaults/ltx2_22B_distilled.json#L14).
- So the accurate answer is: `97` makes sense as a default because it is valid `8N+1`, but it is not hard-fixed by the worker.

**IC-LoRAs**
- The worker does not treat pose/depth/canny IC-LoRAs as user-managed LoRAs. It auto-selects them from the LTX control mode.
- For distilled LTX, `video_prompt_type` letters drive control selection: `P=pose`, `D=depth`, `E=canny`, and the worker auto-loads the union-control IC-LoRA at fixed multiplier `1.0` in [ltx2.py](/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx2.py#L732).
- That is why there are no separate “IC-LoRA settings” in the worker itself: the intended knob is control mode, not picking LoRA files.
- Important limitation: this is effectively a distilled-only path. The handler only exposes LTX control-video choices for distilled in [ltx2_handler.py](/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx2_handler.py#L181), and `get_loras_transformer()` exits early for non-distilled in [ltx2.py](/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx2.py#L740). So full `ltx2_22B` is not really the IC-LoRA control path here.

**What’s Missing**
- The shared travel abstraction is centered on `structure_guidance`, not LTX-specific control modes. In the worker that abstraction only knows `flow`, `canny`, `depth`, `none/raw`, and `uni3c` in [structure_guidance.py](/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/source/core/params/structure_guidance.py#L93).
- That means `pose` is not representable there at all, even though distilled LTX supports it via `PVG`. So pose control is a real missing concept in the current shared travel contract.
- There also appears to be a wiring gap for LTX travel: I did not find code on this branch that maps `structure_guidance.preprocessing` to the LTX control letters (`PVG` / `DVG` / `EVG` / `VG`). The segment processor only uses an explicit `guide_preprocessing` override if it is already present in [segment_processor.py](/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/source/task_handlers/travel/segment_processor.py#L151), and I did not find the orchestrator setting that field.
- On the frontend side, your current travel types only expose `uni3c | flow | canny | depth`, not `pose` or `raw`, in [mediaHandlers.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/types/mediaHandlers.ts#L4) and [structureGuidance.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/tasks/structureGuidance.ts#L11).

So the practical conclusion is:
- `97` is not fixed.
- “No IC-LoRA settings” is partly intentional, because the worker auto-loads them from control mode.
- But there is also a genuine product/integration gap: the current travel contract/UI does not cleanly model LTX-specific control modes, especially `pose` and `raw`, and I do not see the travel worker wiring that turns generic `structure_guidance` into LTX distilled control selection.

If you want, I can do the next pass as a concrete integration spec: exactly what fields the client should send and exactly where the worker should translate them for `ltx2_22B_distilled`.

Can you share a plan for what you think we need from a worker side please?

> AGENT

Yes. From the worker side, I think the plan should be:

1. Define an explicit LTX control-mode contract.
Right now travel has generic `structure_guidance`, but LTX control actually runs off `video_prompt_type` letters. Add one canonical field the worker understands for LTX travel, ideally inside `structure_guidance`, such as `control_mode: 'pose' | 'depth' | 'canny' | 'raw' | 'uni3c' | 'none'`. The parsing entry points are [structure_guidance.py](/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/source/core/params/structure_guidance.py) and [segment_processor.py](/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/source/task_handlers/travel/segment_processor.py).

2. Add a model capability matrix and validate against it.
The worker should stop guessing. It should explicitly say:
- `ltx2_22B_distilled`: supports `pose`, `depth`, `canny`, `raw`, `uni3c`, plain i2v
- `ltx2_22B`: supports plain i2v and probably `uni3c`, but not IC-LoRA control-video modes
- WAN/VACE models keep their current structure-guidance behavior
This validation belongs near [ltx2_handler.py](/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx2_handler.py) and the travel segment build path in [task_registry.py](/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py).

3. Make travel derive `video_prompt_type` from that contract.
In [segment_processor.py](/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/source/task_handlers/travel/segment_processor.py), replace the current implicit LTX path with deterministic mapping:
- `pose` -> `PVG`
- `depth` -> `DVG`
- `canny` -> `EVG`
- `raw` -> `VG`
- `uni3c` -> no `V`-based LTX control mode; let Uni3C injection handle guidance
- `none` -> `S`
That is the missing bridge today.

4. Reject unsupported or ambiguous combinations instead of silently falling back.
Examples:
- LTX + `flow` should error, not quietly behave like start-image mode
- full `ltx2_22B` + `pose/depth/canny/raw` should error
- control mode requiring guide video should error if no guide exists
This should happen before queue submission in [task_registry.py](/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py).

5. Keep IC-LoRA loading automatic, but log it clearly.
The existing auto-load behavior in [ltx2.py](/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx2.py) is fine. What’s missing is better observability: log effective `control_mode`, derived `video_prompt_type`, and whether union-control was activated.

6. Add matrix tests for travel.
At minimum:
- distilled LTX + `pose`
- distilled LTX + `depth`
- distilled LTX + `canny`
- distilled LTX + `raw`
- distilled/full LTX + invalid mode
- LTX segment frame quantization showing lengths are valid `8N+1`, not fixed at `97`
The best places are worker tests around [segment_processor.py](/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/source/task_handlers/travel/segment_processor.py), [task_registry.py](/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py), and the travel orchestrator in [orchestrator.py](/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/source/task_handlers/travel/orchestrator.py).

If you want the shortest practical version: the worker needs one explicit LTX travel control field, one capability matrix, one deterministic mapper to `PVG/DVG/EVG/VG/S`, and hard validation. That would close the current ambiguity.

> DEVELOPER

And will we need to make any changes on the client-side here?

> AGENT

Yes. If the worker takes the clean split above, the client needs corresponding API and UI changes. Not a huge rewrite, but definitely not zero.

The main client change is to stop modeling travel guidance as one generic `structureType` enum for every model. Right now the client still assumes a shared shape around `structure_guidance` and `structureType` in places like [structureGuidance.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/tasks/structureGuidance.ts), [primaryStructureVideo.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/tasks/travelBetweenImages/primaryStructureVideo.ts), and [mediaHandlers.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/types/mediaHandlers.ts). That works for WAN-style guidance, but it does not cleanly represent LTX control modes.

What the client should change:
- Replace the current shared travel guidance model with the same discriminated split as the worker, for example `travelGuidance.kind = 'none' | 'wan_structure' | 'ltx_control' | 'uni3c'`.
- Gate available options by selected model:
  - WAN: `none`, WAN structure modes, `uni3c`
  - LTX distilled: `none`, LTX control modes, `uni3c`
  - LTX full: `none`, `uni3c`
- Remove the assumption that `flow/canny/depth` are universally valid structure modes.
- Add LTX-only UI options for `pose`, `depth`, `canny`, `raw`.
- Keep WAN’s existing structure-video controls on their own branch.
- Update payload builders so travel requests send `travel_guidance`, not the current generic `structure_guidance`, in [payloadBuilder.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/tasks/travelBetweenImages/payloadBuilder.ts) and the segment payload path in [segmentTaskPayload.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/tasks/segmentTaskPayload.ts).

One subtle client-side point: the current batch guidance UI already hides too much and still carries a generic `structureType` value through the editor, for example [BatchGuidanceVideo.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/BatchGuidanceVideo.tsx) and [BatchModeContent.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/ShotImagesEditor/components/BatchModeContent.tsx). That will need to become model-aware rather than just hiding the settings.

So the short answer is: yes, client work is required. The worker split is only clean if the client mirrors it with a family-aware `travelGuidance` model and only exposes valid options per model.

> DEVELOPER

I'm implementing this plan on another thread, can you make a plan for what us? but first, answer these questions:  1. Scope boundary unclear. StructureGuidanceConfig is used by both travel (orchestrator.py,          
  guide_builder.py) AND non-travel tasks (task_registry.py:_process_structure_guidance()). Does
  travel_guidance only replace the travel API surface, with StructureGuidanceConfig still living       
  underneath? Or are you replacing the internal config class too? The plan reads as travel-API-only,
  which would mean StructureGuidanceConfig survives as the internal representation — that's probably
  right, but worth being explicit.
  2. Breaking change with no compat layer. "Do not keep structure_guidance as a supported travel API
  shape" — who are the callers? If this is your own frontend only, fine. If there are external         
  consumers or the Supabase queue has in-flight tasks using the old shape, you need a migration story.
  3. wan_structure vs vace naming. The codebase consistently calls this target "vace" (the target field
   in StructureGuidanceConfig, is_vace property, etc.). Renaming to wan_structure at the API level     
  while the internals say "vace" could be confusing. Consider whether this is genuinely a "WAN
  structure" concept or a "VACE preprocessing" concept — they're the same today but might not be if    
  non-VACE WAN models get structure guidance.           
  4. What happens to StructureGuidanceConfig? The plan says "Create a new family-aware travel guidance
  parser/config" living "around structure_guidance.py." Is this a new class that wraps/replaces        
  StructureGuidanceConfig, or a layer on top that delegates to it? The current class has significant
  logic (multi-source compositing, timeline calculation, legacy compat) that you don't want to rewrite.
  5. ltx_control.mode: 'raw' maps to VG, but VG is also the current default for LTX guide video. In
  segment_processor.py:145, LTX-2 defaults to "VG" when a guide is present regardless of mode. Is raw  
  actually a distinct mode, or is it "no preprocessing, just use VG"? If the latter, maybe omit raw and
   make VG the default when no specific mode is requested.                                             
  6. 97-frame default. The plan mentions "segment frame requests preserve arbitrary valid lengths after
   model quantization instead of defaulting to 97." I didn't find 97 hardcoded as a default —          
  _quantize_frames() in orchestrator.py:45-66 quantizes to step*N + 1. Is 97 coming from somewhere
  specific, or is this aspirational?                                                                   
  7. Missing: Uni3C fields on wan_structure / ltx_control. The plan shows Uni3C has step_window,
  frame_policy, zero_empty_frames, keep_on_gpu — but these are currently configurable via              
  _apply_uni3c_config. Are these fields only on the uni3c kind, or could they also appear on
  wan_structure/ltx_control? The current code treats Uni3C as a separate target, so uni3c-only is      
  correct, just confirming.

> AGENT

1. `travel_guidance` should replace only the travel API surface, not the shared internal config immediately.
`StructureGuidanceConfig` should survive underneath for the WAN/VACE and Uni3C branches. That is the safer boundary. Travel needs a new top-level family-aware contract, but the existing internal class still has real value for multi-source videos, compositing, timeline offsets, and Uni3C/VACE parameter normalization in [structure_guidance.py](/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/source/core/params/structure_guidance.py). So the intended split should be:
- external travel API: new `travel_guidance`
- internal WAN/Uni3C normalization: still `StructureGuidanceConfig`
- internal LTX control branch: separate path, because `pose` does not fit that class cleanly

2. The breaking-change point is valid.
If there are any in-flight queued tasks or any non-frontend callers, removing `structure_guidance` outright is too aggressive. My recommendation is:
- accept both `travel_guidance` and legacy `structure_guidance` in the worker for one transition window
- normalize legacy travel payloads into the new internal travel-guidance model
- make the frontend emit only `travel_guidance`
So I would revise the plan: new shape becomes canonical immediately, but the worker should carry a short-lived compat parser until old tasks are drained.

3. `wan_structure` is probably the wrong name.
You’re right that the internals are really about VACE-style preprocessing, not “WAN” generically. Since non-VACE WAN models may diverge later, I would rename that branch to `vace` or `vace_structure`, not `wan_structure`. My preferred shape is:
- `none`
- `vace`
- `ltx_control`
- `uni3c`
That matches the codebase better and avoids pretending every WAN-family model shares the same guidance semantics.

4. `StructureGuidanceConfig` should be delegated to, not replaced.
I would not rewrite it. The cleaner design is:
- add a new `TravelGuidanceConfig` or equivalent travel-only parser at the API boundary
- if `kind === 'vace'` or `kind === 'uni3c'`, convert into `StructureGuidanceConfig`
- if `kind === 'ltx_control'`, bypass `StructureGuidanceConfig` and go down an LTX-specific control-video path
So this is a wrapper/dispatcher layer, not a replacement.

5. `raw` is distinct conceptually, even if it currently lands on `VG`.
The distinction is not that the letters differ today; it is that the user explicitly requested raw control-video semantics instead of pose/depth/canny. In worker terms, yes, `raw` for LTX distilled currently maps to `VG`, and `VG` is also the present “guide present” behavior in [segment_processor.py](/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/source/task_handlers/travel/segment_processor.py#L142). I would still keep `raw` as an explicit mode in the new API, because otherwise there is no way to distinguish:
- “user intentionally chose raw control”
- “worker fell back to generic guide-video mode”
So API-level `raw` is useful even if runtime mapping stays `VG`.

6. `97` is not from travel quantization logic.
You’re correct that `_quantize_frames()` is not defaulting to `97`. The `97` comes from defaults/fallbacks outside the quantizer:
- generic WGP UI fallback for non-WAN models in [wgp.py](/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/Wan2GP/wgp.py#L10337)
- current frontend defaults for LTX in [settings.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/settings.ts#L104)
So the plan wording should be tightened to: “travel must continue honoring requested lengths after quantization, rather than assuming the common LTX default of `97`.”

Yes, only the `uni3c` branch should carry `step_window`, `frame_policy`, `zero_empty_frames`, and `keep_on_gpu`. They should not appear on `vace` or `ltx_control`. That matches current semantics in [_apply_uni3c_config()](\/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py#L912) and keeps the branches clean.

- worker supports a temporary legacy `structure_guidance` compat layer
- LTX `raw` remains explicit at API level even though it maps to `VG`

> AGENT

Most of those are not bullshit. A few are real corrections, and they improve the plan.

**What I’d change**
1. `treatment` should stay per-video.
The current system already models it per entry via `StructureVideoEntry`, so moving it to kind-level would be a regression for multi-source guidance. The plan should keep `treatment` on each `videos[]` item, not on `vace` / `ltx_control` / `uni3c`.

2. `raw` on `ltx_control` is ambiguous naming.
You’re right that `vace.raw` and `ltx_control.raw` mean different things. I would rename the LTX mode to `video` instead of `raw`.
So:
- `vace.mode`: `flow | canny | depth | raw`
- `ltx_control.mode`: `pose | depth | canny | video`
with `video -> VG`

3. full LTX + Uni3C should be treated conservatively.
I do not have enough evidence that full `ltx2_22B` + Uni3C is truly supported in this travel path. The safer decision-complete plan is:
- `ltx2_22B_distilled`: `none | ltx_control | uni3c`
- full `ltx2_22B`: `none` only
If full LTX + Uni3C is later validated, it can be added deliberately.

4. `ltx_control + uni3c` should not be combinable in v1.
The single-kind shape should make that impossible on purpose. Combining them would create a second axis of guidance composition and reopen ambiguity around prompt type, guide ownership, and double-feeding. For this plan, guidance is mutually exclusive:
- exactly one of `none | vace | ltx_control | uni3c`

5. `uni3c` should imply raw guide video, with no preprocessing field.
Yes, Uni3C should always use raw guide frames in this new split. It should not carry a separate mode/preprocessing field. That is actually part of the cleanup: Uni3C stops pretending to share the VACE preprocessing model.

6. legacy removal should be only at the travel boundary.
Agreed. Do not rip legacy support out of `StructureGuidanceConfig.from_params()` globally. The removal is:
- travel API no longer accepts legacy `structure_guidance`
- internal/non-travel callers can keep using `StructureGuidanceConfig.from_params()` and its legacy support
That is much safer.

7. `videos` should be required by schema shape, then validated at runtime too.
So for `vace`, `ltx_control`, and `uni3c`, `videos` is required in the contract. The worker should still runtime-validate non-empty arrays and usable paths.

> AGENT

Most of these are good catches. The important ones are `OrchestratorDetails` cleanup, segment-level shape, and Uni3C API surface. I’d revise the plan accordingly.

1. Shared `StructureGuidanceConfig` for Uni3C is acceptable in v1.
I agree the name becomes slightly misleading, but I would not split or rename that class in the same change. It is already the internal holder for both VACE and Uni3C semantics. The cleaner move is:
- keep `StructureGuidanceConfig` internal as-is
- make `travel_guidance` the external truth
- let the dispatcher convert `travel_guidance.kind === 'vace' | 'uni3c'` into `StructureGuidanceConfig`
So “own branch” should mean API and control-flow branch, not necessarily a separate class.

2. `ltx_control.video` does need explicit contract docs.
Yes. The conceptual difference is:
- `vace.raw`: use the VACE/WAN structure-guidance path with no preprocessing
- `ltx_control.video`: use LTX’s native video-guide path (`VG`)
Same source material, different downstream engine and prompt semantics. That should be documented in the contract and surfaced in model-gated UI labels, otherwise users will pick the wrong branch.

3. `strength` should validate differently per kind.
Agreed. Keep the wire name if desired, but validation and help text should be per-branch:
- `vace.strength`: VACE motion-strength semantics
- `uni3c.strength`: Uni3C weight semantics
They should not share the same validation assumptions.

4. Old travel fields should be removed from travel `orchestrator_details`.
This is important. For travel payloads, once `travel_guidance` exists:
- do not emit `structure_guidance`, `structure_videos`, `structure_type`, `structure_video_path`, or related legacy travel guidance fields
- treat `travel_guidance` as the single source of truth
- if both old and new fields are somehow present inside travel code, fail fast rather than merge
That avoids silent precedence bugs.

5. Segment-level shape needs to be explicit.
Yes. Each segment payload should receive a resolved `travel_guidance` object, not just inherit the orchestrator version blindly.
The segment shape should be:
- same `kind`
- same user-selected mode/parameters
- resolved `videos` or resolved per-segment guide reference
- internal resolved fields for segment execution where needed, such as `_guidance_video_url` and `_frame_offset` on branches that use them
So the orchestrator owns stitched/guidance timeline math, and segment tasks receive already-resolved per-segment guidance inputs.

6. Uni3C inference knobs should be optional, not required.
I agree. They are too implementation-specific to force every caller to understand them.
Better shape:
- `uni3c` requires only `videos` and optionally `strength`
- `step_window`, `frame_policy`, `zero_empty_frames`, `keep_on_gpu` are optional overrides with worker defaults
That keeps the API smaller while preserving escape hatches.

7. Missing test: distilled LTX + Uni3C.
Correct. Since the validation rules allow it, it needs explicit test coverage.

> AGENT

Most of that is valid. The biggest corrections are: LTX control prompt modes are net-new worker feature work, parsing should stay out of `StructureGuidanceConfig.from_params()`, and the LTX execution path needs a clearer code path.

Here’s how I’d resolve each point:

1. `PVG` / `DVG` / `EVG` are new travel feature work.
Correct. The worker currently only emits `S` and `VG` in the LTX travel path in [segment_processor.py](/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/source/task_handlers/travel/segment_processor.py#L138). The plan should explicitly say this is not just a contract refactor; it adds new LTX travel prompt-mode generation. The reason it is still grounded is that the underlying LTX/WGP stack already advertises those modes in [ltx2_handler.py](/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx2_handler.py#L181) and already maps `P/D/E` to control LoRA behavior in [ltx2.py](/tmp/reigh-worker-check.BazBg2/Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx2.py#L732). What is missing today is the travel-layer bridge.

2. `StructureGuidanceConfig.from_params()` should not grow a third travel path.
I would keep travel parsing separate. The cleaner structure is:
- `TravelGuidanceConfig.from_payload(...)` or equivalent new dispatcher for travel only
- that dispatcher converts `kind: 'vace' | 'uni3c'` into `StructureGuidanceConfig`
- `StructureGuidanceConfig.from_params()` remains for existing non-travel `structure_guidance` and legacy scattered fields
That avoids turning one already-overloaded parser into an even more overloaded one.

3. `ltx_control` needs an explicit parallel prep path.
Agreed. The plan should say where it lives:
- orchestrator still owns multi-video composition, per-video treatment, stitched/guidance offsets, and segment slicing
- for `ltx_control`, it uses the existing video loading/compositing primitives but skips structure preprocessing
- the result is a raw resolved guide video per segment plus resolved frame offset metadata
- segment processor then maps `ltx_control.mode` to `PVG/DVG/EVG/VG`
So this is a parallel branch in travel orchestration/segment prep, not an extension of `StructureGuidanceConfig`.

4. Uni3C `V`-exclusion is preservation, not new behavior.
Yes. That item belongs in the test plan as regression coverage, not in the implementation plan as net-new logic.

5. Test plan gaps are real.
I would add:
- explicit `kind: 'none'` tests
- orchestrator payload -> resolved segment payload tests
- invalid `vace` on full LTX tests
- single-video and multi-video guidance resolution tests
- distilled LTX + Uni3C positive test
- full LTX + any non-`none` guidance negative tests

6. Contract nits:
- `strength` can stay unified intentionally, but the plan should say it is a shared wire name with branch-specific semantics and validation
- `treatment` should stay `adjust | clip`; that matches current `StructureVideoEntry` typing, so I don’t see evidence of more values
- `frame_policy` should be an enum in the API, not a free string, matching the worker’s accepted values

7. full `ltx2_22B` being `none`-only needs justification.
Yes. The rationale should be explicit: full LTX still remains useful for plain start/end-image travel generation, but the new control-guidance feature is scoped to the distilled control-video path because that is the path the worker currently exposes and the only one with IC-LoRA-driven control semantics. So full LTX is still valid for higher-quality plain travel, just not guided travel in this change.

> AGENT

Broadly, the client plan is compatible with `immutable-honking-ladybug.md`, but there are a few places I would tighten so the two plans don’t drift.

Main alignment:
- Both use `travel_guidance` as the canonical travel contract.
- Both make guidance mutually exclusive: `none | vace | ltx_control | uni3c`.
- Both keep `StructureGuidanceConfig` as an internal worker concern, not a client concern.
- Both treat full LTX as unguided for now and distilled LTX as the only LTX control branch.
- Both preserve per-video `treatment`.

The main mismatches:
- The ladybug plan assumes worker-side read strictness immediately: no legacy travel fields, hard exclusivity. The client plan should mirror that more explicitly by saying the payload builder must emit `travel_guidance` only and must not send legacy travel guidance fields under any circumstances.
- The worker plan treats `ltx_control.video` as a first-class mode. The client plan should call that mode out explicitly in the UI spec, not just say “LTX-only controls,” otherwise implementers may forget the raw-video/native-guide option.
- The worker plan makes `strength` meaningful on `ltx_control` too. My client plan currently under-specifies that. If the worker thread keeps that behavior, the client plan should decide whether to expose LTX control strength now or deliberately hide it and rely on worker defaults.
- The worker plan includes segment-level resolved `travel_guidance`. The client plan should state that both main travel generation and segment regeneration must derive from the same canonical `TravelGuidance` state shape, so segment regen does not accidentally keep old `structure_guidance` plumbing.

One bigger architectural concern:
- The ladybug plan adds a pose preprocessor on the worker and keeps LTX VPT at `VG` for all `ltx_control` modes because preprocessing happens before segment execution.
- That means the client should not model LTX as “sending PVG/DVG/EVG.” It should model user intent only: `pose/depth/canny/video`. The payload contract should stay semantic, not letter-based. My client plan is already mostly doing that, but I’d make it explicit so nobody leaks worker prompt letters into the dashboard code.

What I would add to the client plan to match it cleanly:
- Outgoing travel payloads must include `travel_guidance` only, never `structure_guidance`, `structure_videos`, `structure_type`, or similar legacy travel fields.
- Distilled LTX guidance UI must include `video` alongside `pose`, `depth`, and `canny`.
- Decide explicitly whether `ltx_control.strength` is exposed in v1 or intentionally hidden behind worker defaults.
- Segment regeneration must use the same canonical `TravelGuidance` view model and builder as orchestrator generation.
- Historical task read-compat is fine for UI hydration, but once normalized, all saves/regens must round-trip through `TravelGuidance` only.

So the answer is: yes, the client plan is directionally consistent with the ladybug plan, but it should be tightened around `ltx_control.video`, `ltx_control.strength`, strict payload emission, and segment-regeneration reuse. Those are the main places I’d adjust before handing it off.

And what about individual travel segments?

> AGENT

Yes, that needs to be explicit. Individual travel segments are one of the main places this can quietly break.

The rule should be: `individual_travel_segment` must use the same canonical `TravelGuidance` shape and the same normalization/build pipeline as the main travel orchestrator flow. It should not keep its own legacy structure-guidance path.

What that means on the client side:
- The segment regeneration input model should carry `travelGuidance?: TravelGuidance`, not `structure_guidance` or loose `structureType` fields.
- When segment regen is launched from an existing travel generation, the client should resolve the current shot/editor state into canonical `TravelGuidance` first, then pass that into the segment task builder.
- The segment task builder in [segmentTaskPayload.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/tasks/segmentTaskPayload.ts) and the family entrypoint in [families/individualTravelSegment/index.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/tasks/families/individualTravelSegment/index.ts) should emit `travel_guidance` only.
- Historical segment/task data can still be read from old fields, but once loaded it should normalize into `TravelGuidance` immediately, same as the main orchestrator flow.

I would add this directly to the plan as a dedicated section:

- `individual_travel_segment` uses the same canonical `TravelGuidance` type as orchestrator generation.
- Segment regeneration must reuse the same normalization and payload-building helpers as the main travel request path.
- No separate legacy write path is allowed for segment regen.
- Tests must cover segment regen for `none`, `vace`, `ltx_control`, and `uni3c`, plus model-switch coercion before segment submission.

So the short answer is: individual travel segments are not a side case; they should be treated as first-class consumers of the exact same `TravelGuidance` contract.

Implemented the client-side `travel_guidance` migration across the live write paths and the task read adapters.

The main changes are in [travelGuidance.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/tasks/travelGuidance.ts), [payloadBuilder.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/tasks/travelBetweenImages/payloadBuilder.ts), [generateVideoService.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/ShotEditor/services/generateVideoService.ts), [segmentTaskPayload.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/tasks/segmentTaskPayload.ts), and [submitSegmentTask.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/domains/media-lightbox/components/submitSegmentTask.ts). Travel generation and `individual_travel_segment` now both emit canonical `travel_guidance`, and the segment form path in [SegmentRegenerateForm.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/domains/media-lightbox/components/SegmentRegenerateForm.tsx) and [SegmentSlotFormView.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/domains/media-lightbox/components/SegmentSlotFormView.tsx) uses the same normalization path. I also updated the read side in [travelContractData.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/tasks/travelContractData.ts), [structureState.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/tasks/travelBetweenImages/structureState.ts), and [segmentStateResolvers.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/tasks/segmentStateResolvers.ts) so new payloads still hydrate into the existing UI cleanly.

This keeps the UI stable without forcing a full presentation-layer rewrite right now: current editor/task-detail surfaces can still consume structure-style view state, but new writes round-trip through canonical `travel_guidance`.

Verified:
- `npx tsc --noEmit`
- `npx vitest run --config config/testing/vitest.config.ts src/domains/media-lightbox/components/__tests__/submitSegmentTask.test.ts src/shared/lib/tasks/families/individualTravelSegment/index.test.ts src/shared/lib/tasks/travelBetweenImages/__tests__/payloadBuilder.test.ts src/shared/lib/tasks/segmentTaskPayload.test.ts src/shared/components/TaskDetails/hooks/useVideoTravelTaskData.test.ts`
- `npx vitest run --config config/testing/vitest.config.ts src/tools/travel-between-images/components/ShotEditor/services/__tests__/generateVideoService.test.ts src/tools/travel-between-images/components/ShotEditor/services/applySettings/taskDataService.test.ts`

I did not run the full repo test suite.

> AGENT

I’m broadening the shared helper now so the app has one canonical answer for three questions: “what guidance modes are valid for this model?”, “how does the UI represent them?”, and “how do we serialize them back to `travel_guidance`?” That removes the current split where payload builders understand LTX better than the editor does.

> AGENT

The segment forms need the same semantic model as the main editor, otherwise LTX pose/video control would still get collapsed back into legacy VACE semantics. I’m removing that last segment-form mismatch now by making the shared submit helper build canonical `travel_guidance` directly.

> AGENT

The last thing I’m tightening is draft preservation across model switches. Without that, the payloads are right but the editor still degrades UX by collapsing one family’s guidance choice when you hop to another. I’m storing the current model’s canonical guidance separately so WAN and LTX can each keep their own draft state.

> AGENT

Implemented the client-side `travel_guidance` plan end to end in this repo.

The main changes are:
- Canonical travel-guidance helpers and model validation in [travelGuidance.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/tasks/travelGuidance.ts) and new coverage in [travelGuidance.test.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/tasks/travelGuidance.test.ts).
- Main travel generation and individual segment regeneration now both write `travel_guidance` through the same path in [generateVideoService.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/ShotEditor/services/generateVideoService.ts), [segmentTaskPayload.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/tasks/segmentTaskPayload.ts), [segmentSettingsUtils.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/SegmentSettingsForm/segmentSettingsUtils.ts), and [submitSegmentTask.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/domains/media-lightbox/components/submitSegmentTask.ts).
- The editor state now understands model-aware guidance, persists canonical guidance drafts per model, and exposes distilled-LTX guidance modes in [useStructureVideo.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/ShotEditor/hooks/video/useStructureVideo.ts), [useStructureVideoHandlers.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/ShotEditor/hooks/video/useStructureVideoHandlers.ts), and [BatchModeContent.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/ShotEditor/sections/generation/BatchModeContent.tsx).
- Read/hydration paths now understand `travel_guidance` while preserving legacy read support in [travelContractData.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/tasks/travelContractData.ts), [structureState.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/tasks/travelBetweenImages/structureState.ts), and [useVideoRegenerateMode.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/useVideoRegenerateMode.ts).

Verification:
- `npx tsc --noEmit`
- Focused regression slice passed: `8` files, `200` tests
- I also did a write-path audit; remaining `structure_guidance` / `structure_videos` references in non-test code are now read-compat or local shot-setting metadata, not legacy travel task writes

I did not run the full repo test suite.

> AGENT

I wasn’t fully comfortable calling it 100% done before this pass. I found one real issue and fixed it: the canonical guidance sanitizer could overwrite an explicit `'adjust'` treatment with the fallback treatment in some paths. That’s fixed in [travelGuidance.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/tasks/travelGuidance.ts), with regression coverage in [travelGuidance.test.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/tasks/travelGuidance.test.ts). I also tightened the exported batch-travel write surface so the canonical request type no longer advertises legacy structure fields in [taskTypes.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/tasks/travelBetweenImages/taskTypes.ts), and cleaned stale migration wording in [payloadBuilder.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/tasks/travelBetweenImages/payloadBuilder.ts), [uiTypes.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/tasks/travelBetweenImages/uiTypes.ts), and [generateVideoService.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/ShotEditor/services/generateVideoService.ts).

Top-to-bottom sense-check result: the active write paths are aligned. Main travel generation writes canonical `travel_guidance`, the individual segment form path builds canonical guidance before submission, and historical/legacy shapes remain on the read/normalization side rather than the new write path. I did not find another active-path bug in the batch editor or segment regeneration flow.

Verification:
- `npx tsc --noEmit` passed.
- Focused regression slice passed: `8` files, `201` tests.
- I also ran a targeted `desloppify` scan over the touched travel files; it found no structural/coupling issues before the tool crashed on its own JSON serialization bug.

So the scoped client-side plan is now complete from my side. The only caveat is that I still did not run the full repo test suite.

> AGENT

I validated it against the worker docs/code in `Headless-Wan2GP`, and the copy is accurate for the current worker contract.

- Full `ltx2_22B` is `none`-only right now. The worker’s model gate in [travel_guidance.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/core/params/travel_guidance.py) returns `{"none"}` for non-distilled LTX, and the worker test suite explicitly rejects `ltx_control` on full LTX in [test_travel_guidance_config.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/tests/test_travel_guidance_config.py).
- Distilled `ltx2_22B_distilled` supports `ltx_control` plus `uni3c`. The same files show `ltx_control` modes `pose | depth | canny | video`, and [test_travel_ltx_vpt.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/tests/test_travel_ltx_vpt.py) covers that path.
- WAN should not be `uni3c`-only. In the worker, non-LTX models allow `{"none", "vace", "uni3c"}` in [travel_guidance.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/core/params/travel_guidance.py), and [test_travel_guidance_config.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/tests/test_travel_guidance_config.py) explicitly round-trips WAN/VACE modes `flow`, `canny`, `depth`, and `raw`.

So the real issue is naming, not capability:
- `WAN 2.2` in the client is acting like the non-LTX travel family bucket, which includes VACE-style structure guidance plus Uni3C.
- If that feels misleading, the better fix is to relabel that branch as something like `WAN / VACE` or `WAN Structure`, not to collapse it to `uni3c` only.

> DEVELOPER

So should we fix it here or there?

> AGENT

Here, on the client copy.

The worker is already explicit and internally consistent:
- full LTX: unguided only
- distilled LTX: `ltx_control` + `uni3c`
- non-LTX/WAN-family: `vace` + `uni3c`

So I would not change worker behavior for this question. The fix is to make the client language and labels match that reality.

What I’d change here:
- Keep the full-LTX warning, but make it more precise:
  - `Full LTX currently supports unguided travel only. Use WAN/VACE for flow, depth, canny, or raw guidance, or use LTX 2.3 Distilled for pose, depth, canny, or video guidance.`
- Rename the `WAN 2.2` guidance branch in the UI so it does not imply “just plain WAN i2v”.
  - Best options: `WAN / VACE` or `WAN Structure`
- If you want the cleanest UX, also rename the guidance section labels so users see:
  - `Structure Guidance` for WAN/VACE modes
  - `Control Guidance` for distilled LTX modes

So: no worker fix needed for this point. Client copy and labeling should be corrected.

> AGENT

It wasn’t showing because the individual segment form never actually had a model selector wired in. `modelName` was only being passed through as a read-only string for preset/guidance inference in [SegmentSettingsForm.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/SegmentSettingsForm/SegmentSettingsForm.tsx), and the segment task path wasn’t carrying a mutable `model_name` override anyway.

I fixed that end to end. The segment form now renders the same travel model pills, the selection is owned in [SegmentRegenerateForm.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/domains/media-lightbox/components/SegmentRegenerateForm.tsx) and [SegmentSlotFormView.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/domains/media-lightbox/components/SegmentSlotFormView.tsx), and the chosen model is written into segment task params through [segmentSettingsUtils.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/SegmentSettingsForm/segmentSettingsUtils.ts), [submitSegmentTask.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/domains/media-lightbox/components/submitSegmentTask.ts), and respected by [segmentStateResolvers.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/tasks/segmentStateResolvers.ts). I also added the full-LTX unguided warning on the segment form when a structure video is present.

Verification passed:
- `npx tsc --noEmit`
- focused tests:
  - [SegmentSettingsForm.test.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/SegmentSettingsForm/SegmentSettingsForm.test.tsx)
  - [submitSegmentTask.test.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/domains/media-lightbox/components/__tests__/submitSegmentTask.test.ts)
  - [segmentStateResolvers.test.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/tasks/segmentStateResolvers.test.ts)

> AGENT

**Findings**
- The individual segment form does not have a model-aware guidance editor. In [StructureVideoSection.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/SegmentSettingsForm/components/StructureVideoSection.tsx#L174), `structureVideoType` is only displayed as a badge; there is no mode selector like the batch editor has in [BatchModeContent.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/ShotEditor/sections/generation/BatchModeContent.tsx#L227). That is why you only see generic overrides like strength/treatment, not LTX-specific mode controls.
- The segment settings model is too narrow for the current travel feature set. [segmentSettings.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/types/segmentSettings.ts#L24) only stores `structureMotionStrength`, `structureTreatment`, and `structureUni3cEndPercent` for guidance-related overrides. There is no place for selected guidance mode, `guidanceScale`, or any richer LTX-specific settings.
- The batch and segment flows are using different abstractions. Batch owns canonical model-aware guidance state via [useStructureVideo.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/ShotEditor/hooks/video/useStructureVideo.ts#L117), while the segment path still builds guidance late from a reduced override surface in [submitSegmentTask.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/domains/media-lightbox/components/submitSegmentTask.ts#L61). That split is the real design debt.
- The model selector I added to the segment form is currently a bridge, not a full unification. It now changes `model_name` and affects task payloads through [SegmentSettingsForm.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/SegmentSettingsForm/SegmentSettingsForm.tsx#L206) and [segmentSettingsUtils.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/SegmentSettingsForm/segmentSettingsUtils.ts#L189), but the rest of the segment guidance UI is still the old override UI. So yes, the break is not clean yet.

So the direct answer is: it does not show the LTX settings because the segment form still isn’t using the same guidance editor as batch generation. It only exposes the old “structure video overrides” surface, and that surface was never designed for model-family-specific controls.

If you want this to feel clean, the next step is not another small patch. It is to extract one shared `TravelGuidanceEditor` + `TravelModelSelector` view model and use it in both places, instead of having batch own the real travel UI and segment regen own a narrower legacy form.

> AGENT

I’m reviewing the plan file against the current repo shape first. I’ll check whether it matches the actual defaults/override plumbing and call out any gaps before I turn it into an implementation-ready spec.

> AGENT

I sense-checked it against the repo. Direction is right, but it is not execution-ready yet, and I can’t execute it while we’re still in Plan Mode.

Main corrections needed before implementation:
- The plan mixes `SelectedModel` ids and worker `model_name` strings. In this repo, UI state should stay on `SelectedModel` (`wan-2.2`, `ltx-2.3`, `ltx-2.3-fast`) and only convert to worker `model_name` at payload time. The plan’s `modelName?: string` segment override shape is the wrong abstraction.
- `guidanceStrength` is not `0-1` here. Current travel guidance defaults/use are around `0.5`, `1`, `1.2`, and the segment/batch UI strength sliders are effectively `0-2`. The plan should not encode the wrong range.
- `guidanceScale` is not part of the current batch guidance subsection; it lives with model settings in `BatchSettingsForm`. So the shared extraction should be a broader shared “model + guidance” section, not just a guidance-mode editor.
- Shot defaults are already split across two stores for a reason. Segment inheritance has to merge:
  - shot travel defaults from `travel-between-images`
  - shot structure/guidance defaults from `travel-structure-video`
  - segment overrides from `metadata.segmentOverrides`
  The plan hints at this, but the merge order and ownership need to be explicit.
- “Set as Shot Defaults” cannot be a single generic save path yet. Prompt/motion fields already flow through `saveFieldAsDefault`, but structure/guidance defaults currently use a separate structure-settings path. The plan needs a host adapter that preserves that split instead of pretending it is one storage API.
- The plan should explicitly reuse the existing `travel_guidance_by_model` shot-level behavior. Otherwise segment inheritance will drift from batch inheritance when switching models.

The clean version should be: one shared normalized editor state, two underlying shot stores, sparse segment overrides keyed by UI-level fields, and one payload builder. Here is the corrected replacement plan.

> AGENT

Implemented the plan. Batch generation and individual segment regeneration now use the same model-aware travel guidance surface and the same defaults/overrides model.

The main change is the shared editor in [TravelGuidanceEditor.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/travel/TravelGuidanceEditor.tsx), wired into batch in [BatchModeContent.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/ShotEditor/sections/generation/BatchModeContent.tsx) and into the segment structure section in [StructureVideoSection.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/SegmentSettingsForm/components/StructureVideoSection.tsx). Segment settings now carry canonical model/guidance fields in [segmentSettings.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/types/segmentSettings.ts), persist them through [settingsMigration.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/settingsMigration.ts), merge shot defaults plus sparse overrides in [useSegmentSettings.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/hooks/segments/useSegmentSettings.ts), and feed both regenerate paths through the same payload logic in [submitSegmentTask.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/domains/media-lightbox/components/submitSegmentTask.ts). I also fixed the shot-editor batch pair path in [pairPayload.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/ShotEditor/services/generateVideo/pairPayload.ts) so canonical guidance overrides are no longer dropped there.

For model-aware shot defaults, the external regenerate flow now reads and writes `travel_guidance_by_model` in [useVideoRegenerateMode.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/useVideoRegenerateMode.ts), and the shot-editor segment-slot flow now receives the same per-model defaults map via [useStructureVideo.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/ShotEditor/hooks/video/useStructureVideo.ts), [TimelineSection.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/ShotEditor/sections/TimelineSection.tsx), and [useSegmentSlotPresentationAdapter.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/ShotImagesEditor/hooks/useSegmentSlotPresentationAdapter.ts). That closes the old split where segment regen had a reduced legacy guidance UI and stale model handling.

Verification:
- `npx tsc --noEmit`
- Focused regression slice passed: `8` files, `57` tests
- This included batch UI, segment form, segment hook merge logic, regenerate submission, slot presentation, settings migration, and pair-payload coverage

One caveat remains: I did not run the full repo test suite. The only issue seen in the focused pass was a pre-existing React `act(...)` warning in [useSegmentSettingsForm.test.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/hooks/__tests__/useSegmentSettingsForm.test.ts), but it is not a failing test.

> AGENT

The model selector was only showing when a structure video existed because the shared editor was literally nested under `structureVideoType && ...` in [StructureVideoSection.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/SegmentSettingsForm/components/StructureVideoSection.tsx). I moved `TravelGuidanceEditor` outside that gate, so the segment form now always shows model selection, while the guidance-mode controls still stay correctly gated by actual structure-video presence. I also added a regression test in [StructureVideoSection.test.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/SegmentSettingsForm/components/__tests__/StructureVideoSection.test.tsx) to lock down the no-video case.

Validation:
- `npx tsc --noEmit` passed.
- Targeted segment tests passed.
- `npm test` completed and the repo is still not globally green: `12 failed | 819 passed` test files, `6 failed | 4301 passed` tests.
